B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

小山羊检测呈阳性后,北卡罗来纳州 7 个县可能接触 狂犬病

Possible rabies exposure in 7 NC counties after baby goats test positive
新闻简报北卡罗来纳州哈莫尼市一家移动宠物动物园的三只小山羊身上检测到了 狂犬病,该动物园与七个县的居民有过接触。据信,这些山羊在 7 月下旬被臭鼬接触过 狂犬病,第四只山羊尽管没有接受检测,但仍被认为已被感染。官员们正在努力通知可能在 7 月 28 日至 8 月 27 日期间接触过山羊的与会者。北卡罗来纳州卫生与公众服务部指出,人类 狂犬病 感染在美国极为罕见,自 2011 年以来该州没有报告病例。公共卫生官员敦促任何直接接触过受影响动物的人寻求评估并联系当地卫生部门。调查正在进行中,随着情况的发展,预计将公布有关潜在暴露地点的更多细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国卫生与公众服务部于 8 月 28 日发出警告,调查仍在进行中,预计将在调查过程中提供更多信息。
地点与对象
该事件涉及北卡罗来纳州的七个县,受感染的小山羊是北卡罗来纳州哈莫尼市一个移动宠物动物园的一部分,并可能使多个地点的居民受到感染。
事件
在一家流动宠物动物园的三只小山羊身上检测到了 狂犬病,据信是一只臭鼬在 7 月底暴露了它们。第四只山羊没有接受检测,但假设被感染。官员们正在努力通知可能接触过山羊的与会者。
规模、影响与风险
受感染的山羊被带到北卡罗来纳州至少七个县,7月28日至8月27日期间与它们接触过的人可能已经被感染。自 2011 年以来,该州没有报告过人类病例,人类 狂犬病 感染在美国被描述为“极其罕见”。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员正在与活动组织者合作,通知与会者,并鼓励那些可能接触过的人联系当地卫生部门。调查正在进行中,可能会公布更多暴露地点。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Possible rabies exposure in 7 NC counties after baby goats test positive

News Brief
Rabies was detected in three baby goats at a mobile petting zoo in Harmony, North Carolina, which had contact with residents across seven counties. The goats are believed to have been exposed to rabies by a skunk in late July, and the fourth goat is assumed infected despite not being tested. Officials are working to notify attendees who may have had contact with the goats between July 28 and August 27. The N.C. Department of Health and Human Services noted that human rabies infections are extremely rare in the U.S., with no cases reported in the state since 2011. Public health officials are urging anyone who had direct contact with the affected animals to seek evaluation and contact their local health department. The investigation is ongoing, with more details about potential exposure locations expected to be released as the situation develops.
Original source text (English)
Rabies was detected in three baby goats which were in contact with many North Carolina residents through a mobile petting zoo, the N.C. Department of Health and Human Services warned . According to a Friday, Aug. 28 release, the N.C. DHHS and N.C. Department of Agriculture and Consumer Services were first notified of a potential rabies exposure among four baby goats at Farm Adventures in Harmony on Aug. 27. The goats are believed to have been exposed to rabies by a skunk that entered a pen where they were kept in late July. The N.C. State Laboratory of Public Health confirmed rabies in three of the baby goats on Aug. 28. The fourth goat was not tested but is also assumed to have been infected. Officials are working with the farm and impacted local health departments to gather information about potential exposure dates and locations; based on information gathered Aug. 28, it has been determined that no other animals on the farm pose a rabies risk. The infected animals were taken to at least seven NC counties according to latest information from NC agencies. Here's what else to know. Farm Adventures operates a petting zoo in Harmony, as well as off-site mobile petting zoo events. Anyone who had contact with the baby goats at any location between July 28 and Aug. 27 may have been exposed while the animals were infectious. Impacted individuals should contact their local health department to determine whether medications are needed to prevent infection, and are encouraged to call before visiting an emergency department or urgent care clinic to determine if treatment is needed and where it can be accessed. Known exposure locations where the infected baby goats were taken include: Jurney's Assisted Living, 1942 Van Haven Drive, Statesville, Iredell County - July 28 Private event, Mecklenburg County - July 30 Community Connections Back-to-School Bash, Breeden Amphitheater, 333 S. Main St., Lexington, Davidson County - Aug. 1 Brookdale Peachtree, 2806 Peachtree Road, Statesville, Iredell County - Aug. 6 Private event, Watauga County - Aug. 8 Private event in Burke County - Aug. 9 Masonic Picnic Grounds, 201 Poplar St., Mocksville, Davie County - Aug. 15 Private event, Durham County - Aug. 16 Private event, Iredell County - Aug. 22 Public health officials are working with event organizers to notify attendees who may have had contact with the goats. The investigation is ongoing, with additional information about potential exposure locations to be released when available. The N.C. DHHS statement noted that human rabies infections are "extremely rare" in the U.S., and no human cases have been reported in the state since 2011. The N.C. DHHS states that rabies can be spread from wild animals to pets and people primarily through bites, scratches, or infected saliva touching broken skin or mucous membranes. In the case of the infected baby goats, it is possible that people could have been exposed to the virus through saliva from an infected animal. As baby goats are often bottle-fed, handled closely or cuddled, public health officials are "working proactively" to ensure anyone who had direct contact with the affected animals is evaluated. Though any mammal can carry and spread rabies, Mayo Clinic states that pets and farm most likely to transmit the rabies virus include cats, cows, dogs, ferrets, goats and horses. Wild animals most likely to spread the virus include bats, beavers, coyotes, foxes, monkeys, raccoons, skunks and woodchucks. Rabies is almost always fatal in mammals, including humans, unless the proper medications are given before symptoms develop. Symptoms can begin weeks to months after someone is exposed. Once the virus enters the body, it travels along the nerves to the brain. Animals with rabies may show a variety of signs. The American Veterinary Medical Association lists the following symptoms as common: Depression, self-mutilation or increased sensitivity to light in horses and livestock Aggressive behavior or unusually affectionate, friendly or fearless behavior Generally unusual behavior, such as nocturnal animals becoming active during the daytime In humans, early symptoms can be flu-like, including weakness, fever or headache as well as a prickling or itching sensation where someone was bitten or exposed. A full list of rabies symptoms among humans from Mayo Clinic includes: Fear brought on by attempts to drink fluids because of difficulty swallowing water Fear brought on by air blown on the face Iris Seaton is the trending news reporter for the Asheville Citizen Times, part of the USA TODAY Network. Reach her at iseaton@citizentimes.com. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

近 300 人在移动宠物动物园接触过带有 狂犬病 的山羊 - CNN

Nearly 300 people exposed to goats with rabies at mobile petting zoo - CNN
新闻简报美国北卡罗来纳州一家移动宠物动物园因涉及小山羊而导致近300人接触狂犬病。这些山羊属于 Farm Adventures,是 2026 年 7 月 28 日至 8 月 27 日期间访问至少 10 个地点的旅行活动的一部分。当地卫生部门对 284 人进行了评估,其中 213 人接受了 暴露后预防 (PEP)。北卡罗来纳州卫生部门在三只山羊身上确认了 狂犬病,其中一只于 8 月 19 日死亡,另一只于第二天被安乐死。疾病预防控制中心支持风险评估,该州有充足的 PEP 供应。该事件凸显了 狂犬病 的风险,如果没有提示 治疗,这种风险通常是致命的。该农场已对受感染的山羊实施了安乐死,并正在监测其他动物,同时该设施将不再饲养 宿主 农场动物。有关该事件和暴露者的一些细节仍不清楚。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该活动发生于2026年7月28日至8月27日期间,评估持续至2026年8月28日。发布日期为2026年9月4日。
地点与对象
该事件发生在美国北卡罗来纳州,涉及与小羊羔互动的人员在移动式动物接触区。受影响的人群包括由当地卫生部门评估的284人,以及直接咨询医疗保健提供者的其他人。
事件
北卡罗来纳州当地卫生部门在人们与移动宠物动物园的小山羊互动后,对人们进行了 狂犬病 评估。这些山羊被证实患有 狂犬病,并建议 213 只山羊使用 暴露后预防 (PEP)。
规模、影响与风险
北卡罗来纳州卫生部门建议对 213 人进行 PEP,并对 284 人进行了评估。这些山羊是一个移动宠物动物园的一部分,该动物园至少去过 10 个地点。在没有提示 治疗 的情况下,狂犬病 感染的风险被明确说明为可能致命,如果立即启动,PEP 被认为是 100% 有效。
应对、状态与不确定性
北卡罗来纳州卫生部门和 CDC 已协调确定哪些人受到了暴露以及哪些人可能需要 治疗。这些山羊已被安乐死,农场正在监测其他动物。卫生部门拥有充足的预防药物供应,并将提供给任何需要的人。有关该事件和暴露者的一些细节仍不清楚。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nearly 300 people exposed to goats with rabies at mobile petting zoo - CNN

News Brief
A mobile petting zoo in North Carolina, USA, involving baby goats, has exposed nearly 300 people to rabies. The goats, owned by Farm Adventures, were part of a traveling event that visited at least 10 locations between July 28 and August 27, 2026. Local health departments evaluated 284 individuals, with 213 receiving post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP). The North Carolina health department confirmed rabies in three goats, with one dying on August 19 and another being euthanized the next day. The CDC has supported risk assessments, and the state has an adequate supply of PEP. The incident highlights the risk of rabies, which is typically fatal without prompt treatment. The farm has euthanized the infected goats and is monitoring other animals, while the facility will no longer host farm animals. Some details about the event and those exposed remain unclear.
Original source text (English)

300 people exposed to goats with rabies at mobile petting zoo Agriculture Vaccines See all topics Facebook Tweet Email Link Threads Link Copied! The North Carolina health department recommended post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) for 213 of the people who were evaluated for potential rabies risk. BSIP SA/Alamy Stock Photo Local health departments in North Carolina have evaluated hundreds of people for rabies after they interacted with baby goats that were part of a mobile petting zoo, according to the state Department of Health and Human Services. As of Wednesday, 284 people have been evaluated by local health departments, while others have consulted with a healthcare provider directly to assess their potential rabies risk. The goats were owned by Farm Adventures, based in Harmony, North Carolina. Between July 28 and August 27, local officials said in a statement, the farm took the goats throughout the state and visited at least 10 locations, including a Masonic picnic, assisted living centers and a back-to-school bash, as well as private events. Ad Feedback Related article Colorized transmission electron micrograph of rabies virus particles (blue). BSIP SA/Alamy Stock Photo Baby goats with rabies at North Carolina mobile petting zoo prompt calls across multiple counties to contact health officials 5 min read The state health department recommended post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) for 213 of the people who were evaluated. If started promptly after exposure, PEP is considered 100% effective at preventing a fatal rabies infection. About 100,000 people are treated with PEP each year in the US, according to the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. It typically starts with wound care if there was a bite or scratch — rabies can also spread through contact with an animal’s saliva — followed by human rabies immunoglobulin to help fight a potential infection until vaccines take effect. Four or five vaccine doses are given, based on the patient’s weight. In the past, people exposed to rabies received up to 30 painful vaccine shots in the stomach. Today, adults can get the shots in the shoulder muscle, while children can get them in the thigh over the course of 14 days. Side effects are generally mild and may include a sore arm, a headache and an upset stomach. Without prompt treatment, rabies is typically fatal. Symptoms, which can take up to two months to develop, may include headache, nausea and sore throat before progressing to hallucinations, delusions, disorientation, paralysis and coma. Related vertical video Krista Edelman She woke up with a bat on her mouth At Jurney’s Assisted Living Home in Statesville, Executive Director Chrissy Stein-Martin said eight residents are undergoing treatment after interacting with the goats. None refused PEP, she said, and the hospital and health departments have been “great to work with.” “They’ve been asking good questions, seeing what they can do to help. In fact, the health department’s coming here to do all the follow-up vaccines so we don’t have to take the residents out. And the families have been very supportive,” she said. Going forward, Stein-Martin said, the facility will still welcome dog visitors but will no longer host farm animals. Alpacas from a local farm have regularly visited the facility, but when she learned that North Carolina requires rabies vaccines for only cats, dogs and ferrets — not farm animals, including goats – she decided “there’s no reason to put everyone at risk.” Farm Adventures said in a statement that its “4 precious baby goats” that may have exposed people to rabies have been euthanized. Tests detected the virus in three of the goats. The farm said it is monitoring its other animals, but none has become sick. Related vertical video TRIMET These two goats apparently had somewhere to be The baby goats had been kept in a separate pen because they were too young to get vaccinated, according to the farm’s owner, Misty Love, who told CNN on Saturday that a wild skunk had gotten into the baby goats’ pen July 29. The farm shot and burned the skunk. None of the goats appeared to have been bitten, but one died August 19, and another fell ill the next day and had to be euthanized. North Carolina’s health department said it is “well prepared to respond to rabies.” About 300 animals test positive for rabies in the state every year; most of them wild. In 2025, more than 4,000 animals were tested for rabies in North Carolina, and only about 6% were positive for the virus, the health department said. As of August 28, more than 320 people in North Carolina had received PEP, the health department said. That figure covers all rabies exposures in the state, not just people exposed to the infected goats. Since rabies was confirmed in the goats, state and local health authorities and the CDC have coordinated with the farm and local authorities to determine who was exposed and who may need treatment. The state health department said it has been in regular contact with the CDC, and that agency said Monday that it supplied information and tools to support rabies risk assessments. The CDC’s latest rabies exposure surveillance report, released Monday, analyzed cases from 2024 and reported that four people died of rabies that year. Two were exposed to bats and did not receive treatment. Another case involved international travel. There was no information about the fourth case. The state health department said North Carolina has an adequate supply of prophylaxis and would provide it to anyone who needs it. The Mecklenburg County Public Health Department, which had one private event with exposure to the goats, declined to share details about the event or those exposed. Anyone with concerns is urged to contact the department. At Jurney’s, Stein-Martin said, residents have “not been stressed at all” about the experience. Instead, they’ve been making light of it – “maybe even too light,” making goat sounds at each other and joking about not licking people. She said levity is important in a potentially scary situation. In fact, Stein-Martin, who writes psychological thrillers in her spare time, said residents keep encouraging her to write a new book about a rabies scare in an assisted living community. “Unexpectedly, I now feel like I’m 100% qualified to do that,” she said. CNN’s Ray Sanchez and Deidre McPhillips contributed to this report. Agriculture Vaccines See all topics Facebook Tweet Email Link Threads Link Copied! What if a Bat Touched Me? Understanding Rabies Risk and What to Do Next - Everyday Health

News Brief
The article, published on August 31, 2026, by Everyday Health, discusses the risks of rabies from bat encounters. It highlights a case of an 11-year-old boy in Canada who died from rabies after waking up to find a bat on his face, despite no visible bites. The article explains that while touching a bat's fur or contact with its bodily fluids is not considered a rabies exposure on its own, any direct contact with the bat's saliva through a bite, scratch, or contact with the eyes, nose, or mouth is high-risk. It stresses the importance of immediate medical evaluation and the potential to avoid post-exposure treatment if the bat is tested and found negative. The piece also outlines general prevention measures and differentiates between high-risk and low-risk exposures based on the context of the encounter.

Learn what to do if you have a bat encounter. Ivan Kuzmin/Adobe Stock By Korin Miller Published on August 31, 2026 Fact-Checked by Emily Kay Votruba Add us on Google ON THIS PAGE Can Touching a Bat Make You Sick? How to Know if a Bat Exposure Is High-Risk or Low-Risk What to Do After a Bat Touches You Should You Go to the ER? How to Prevent Bat-Related Illness ON THIS PAGE ON THIS PAGE Can Touching a Bat Make You Sick? How to Know if a Bat Exposure Is High-Risk or Low-Risk What to Do After a Bat Touches You Should You Go to the ER? How to Prevent Bat-Related Illness The sight of a bat flying through the sky may be harmless, but an up-close encounter is a different story. Just ask Krista Edelman, MD, a gastroenterologist in Virginia, who recently went viral after revealing in an Instagram video that she woke up to find a bat attempting to burrow into her mouth. Dr. Edelman said in her video that she dreamed a mouse was trying to get into her mouth, only to wake up and discover that it was a bat. “I went from thinking I was dreaming to realizing very quickly that this was, in fact, happening,” she said. [1] Edelman’s video comes just a few weeks after an 11-year-old boy in Canada died after waking up to find a bat on his face. Initially, his family didn’t seek medical care for him because there weren’t any visible bites. But he developed symptoms of rabies about 19 days later, and died after two weeks in the hospital. [2] If you come into contact with a bat, it’s essential to get proper medical assessment and treatment. Doctors explain the health risks of touching a bat. Can Touching a Bat Make You Sick? Rabies, a serious viral disease, is the biggest health concern from bat encounters. These flying mammals are the leading source of human rabies cases in the United States. Of the 60,000 Americans who get rabies shots each year, two-thirds do so because of a bat exposure. Among rabies fatalities, at least 7 out of 10 are caused by bats. [3] [4] But the type of bat exposure matters. “Touching a bat’s fur, or contact with bat blood, urine, or droppings, is not by itself considered a rabies exposure and does not require treatment,” says Zoe Weiss, MD, a clinical microbiologist and an infectious disease specialist at Tufts Medical Center in Boston. The situation is different and far more dangerous if the bat’s saliva enters the body through a bite, scratch, or via contact with the eyes, nose, or mouth, she explains. The problem is that it can be difficult to know if a bat has bitten or scratched you, says Neil Modi, MD, an infectious disease physician at Northwell Health Primary and Specialty Care at Chappaqua Crossing, New York. “Because bat bites and scratches can be easily missed and wounds can often be minuscule, many people do not realize they have been scratched or bitten,” he explains. As a result, “any direct physical contact with a bat should be evaluated by a health professional rather than dismissed,” Dr. Weiss says. Rabies is almost always fatal in humans if it’s not treated promptly after an exposure. But the virus that causes rabies cannot be transmitted from person to person. How to Know if a Bat Exposure Is High-Risk or Low-Risk Healthcare providers typically categorize bat exposures as high-risk or low-risk. High-Risk Bat Exposures Common high-risk exposures are waking up to find a bat flying around your bedroom or a bat flying into you, according to John Crane, MD, PhD, a professor of medicine in the division of infectious diseases at the State University of New York at Buffalo. In this situation, you can’t rule out the possibility that the bat has touched, scratched, or bitten you, he explains. It’s also concerning if a bat flies into you, because it suggests something is wrong with the animal. “Bats have excellent echolocation and navigation skills — they are very adept flyers and are not clumsy when healthy,” says Christopher Bazzoli, MD, an emergency medicine physician at Cleveland Clinic. “A normal bat will not collide with a human, even if inside a home.” Finding a bat near an unattended child is also considered a high-risk exposure if the bat cannot be tested, Dr. Modi says. “As far as your pets, finding a bat near your pet does not necessarily qualify as a human exposure,” he says. "It does qualify as a pet exposure. Your pet should be taken to your veterinarian for a thorough assessment.” Low-Risk Bat Exposures If you see a bat outdoors, it’s considered a low-risk encounter and does not require treatment, Dr. Crane says. “If you had a bat in your garage, opened the door, and the bat flew out, that would be low-risk,” he says. Touching a bat’s fur is considered low-risk as well, as long as you can rule out a bite or scratch, Modi says. But you should never pick up a bat with your bare hands, he notes. What to Do Immediately After a Bat Touches You It is typically considered a high-risk exposure if a bat touches you (even if you can’t see a wound), bites you, or tries to get in your mouth. “Wash the site immediately and thoroughly with soap and water for several minutes,” Weiss says. “Immediate wound washing alone substantially reduces rabies risk.” Consider Trying to Catch the Bat to Be Tested for Rabies If you’re indoors and able to safely capture the bat that touched you, doctors recommend trying to catch it so health officials can test it for rabies. “If the bat can be safely captured and tested, and the result is negative, post-exposure treatment can be avoided entirely, or stopped if already started,” Weiss says. She suggests the following for safety while trying to capture a bat: Wear thick gloves. Do not try to touch the bat with bare hands. Use a box or coffee can. When the bat lands, cover it with a container, slide cardboard underneath to trap the animal and tape the container closed with air holes. Contact local animal control or your local health department. They can arrange testing and determine if treatment is needed. Preserve the bat for testing. “Do not release it and try not to damage the head, which is needed for the test,” Weiss says. “Never attempt to keep or relocate a live bat.” Should You Go to the ER if a Bat Touches You? It is important to seek medical care quickly after a physical encounter with a bat. Weiss suggests contacting your local health department and going to the emergency room for a rabies shot and immunoglobulin, an emergency medicine made from human blood that delivers short-term antibodies to help neutralize the rabies virus. Weiss says it’s better to go to an ER than an urgent care facility, since ERs are more likely to have the treatment you’ll need. “Not all locations will carry the rabies immunoglobulin, so it does also help to either call ahead to the location to check if they have it, or call your local health department. They can usually direct people to a nearby site known to stock rabies treatment, which is often faster than calling around,” she says. During a medical evaluation, your healthcare provider will assess your risk by getting a detailed history of the exposure to determine if treatment is needed, per Modi. “They will also assess the wound and provide local wound care,” he says. “Often, a public health official will be contacted as well to assist with the assessment.” Post-exposure prophylaxis (or PEP), the shots you get to prevent rabies infection, “should begin as soon as possible after exposure, ideally the same day, although it should still be given even after a delay,” Weiss says. “Rabies is essentially fatal once the disease reaches the central nervous system,” says Dr. Bazzoli. “That being said, the process of tissue exposure to infection, and that infection spreading to the central nervous system, takes days — our best data indicate a week or more. This is why we think of rabies exposure as a medical urgency, and typically not an emergency.” Are Rabies Shots Painful? You may have heard that rabies vaccination involves a painful shot in the stomach, but those are a thing of the past. “That hasn’t been the case for more than 20 years, and it’s a dangerous myth,” Crane says. “This is a regular injection in your arm, like a flu shot.” The rabies vaccine is often a series of shots in the arm, according to Modi. “Depending on if you have been vaccinated in the past, the number of shots can range from two to four,” he says. “Additionally, your healthcare provider may determine you need locally administered rabies immunoglobulin, depending on your medical history, vaccination history, and the details of your exposure.” How to Prevent Bat-Related Illness There are a few things you can do to lower your chances of a high-risk bat encounter. Try to keep bats out of your living spaces. That means sealing any potential entry points for wildlife, such as vents, chimneys, or windows, Modi says. Don’t handle bats. “Never directly touch or handle a bat,” Modi says. Vaccinate pets against rabies. This "maintains a barrier between wildlife and people,” Weiss says. Brevard County health officials warn of rabies, West Nile risks - floridatoday.com

时间
狂犬病 警报于 2026 年 9 月 3 日星期四发布,以回应 2026 年 8 月 31 日捕获的浣熊中确诊的 狂犬病 病例。该警报的有效期为 60 天。

佛罗里达州布里瓦德县,特别是安德鲁斯大道附近的墨尔本地区。受影响的人群包括居民、宠物和牲畜。

布里瓦德县卫生官员在一只浣熊病毒检测呈阳性后发布了 狂犬病 警报。他们还警告称,由于哨兵鸡检测呈阳性,存在西尼罗河病毒风险。
规模、影响与风险
该事件涉及浣熊中确诊的 狂犬病 病例和最近哨兵鸡中的西尼罗河病毒病例。卫生官员建议居民避免接触野生动物,并采取预防措施防止蚊虫叮咬,以预防疾病 传播。
应对、状态与不确定性

Brevard County health officials warn of rabies, West Nile risks - floridatoday.com

News Brief
Brevard County health officials issued a rabies alert on September 3, 2026, following a confirmed rabies case in a raccoon captured on August 31 in the Melbourne area. The alert, valid for 60 days, covers a specific geographic boundary within the county. Officials also warned about West Nile virus risks due to positive tests in sentinel chickens. They advised residents to avoid contact with wild animals, secure pets, and take mosquito bite prevention measures. Public education efforts include reporting dead birds and seeking medical attention after potential rabies exposure. State and county authorities are coordinating surveillance and prevention strategies, such as mosquito monitoring and targeted treatments, to mitigate disease spread. The alert emphasizes the importance of post-exposure treatment and highlights the need for vigilance against both rabies and West Nile virus.
Original source text (English)
News Space Sports Restaurants Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals LOCAL Brevard County health officials warn of rabies, West Nile risks Jim Waymer Florida Today Sept. 3, 2026, 12:06 p.m. ET Health officials in Brevard County, Florida, have issued warnings for both West Nile virus and rabies. A rabies alert was issued after a raccoon captured in Melbourne tested positive for the virus. Officials advise residents to avoid wild animals, secure pets, and take precautions against mosquito bites. Florida health officials warned Thursday about risks to Brevard County residents from rabies and the West Nile virus. Several sentinel chickens within the county have recently tested positive for West Nile virus, triggering a health advisory. Thursday's rabies alert was in response to a confirmed case of rabies in a raccoon that was captured in the Melbourne area off Andrews Avenue on Aug. 27. State health officials and Brevard County Mosquito Control are coordinating surveillance and prevention efforts "by enhanced monitoring for mosquitoes and diseases as well as targeted mosquito treatment and overnight spraying to reduce the risk of disease transmission," FDOH said in a release. State and county officials continue to conduct statewide surveillance for mosquito-borne illnesses,including West Nile virus infections, Eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, malaria,chikungunya and dengue. Health officials urge the public to avoid being bitten by mosquitoes by taking basic precautions, such as avoiding being outdoors at dawn and dusk, using mosquito repellants and/or wearing long-sleaves and pants to cover the skin. Residents also are encouraged to report dead birds to the Florida Fish and Wildlife Conservation Commission’s website: myfwc.com/research/wildlife/health/avian. Brevard health officials issue Sept. 3 rabies alert On Thursday (Sept. 3) state health officials issued a rabies alert in Brevard in response to a confirmed case of the viral infection in a raccoon that was captured in the Melbourne area off Andrews Avenue on Aug. 27. "People and domestic animals should always avoid physical contact with wild animals," Florida health officials warned in a press release. That includes raccoons, bats, foxes, skunks, otters, bobcats, coyotes, which carry a higher risk of human exposure and a need for rabies post-exposure treatment. "If you are exposed to rabies, receiving appropriate treatment after exposure will protect you from the risk of rabies." The rabies alert is for 60 days and includes the following boundaries in Brevard County: Northern boundary: West Eau Gallie Boulevard Southern boundary: Sarno Road Eastern boundary: Croton Road Western boundary: North Wickham Road Immunize your pets and livestock based on your veterinarian’s recommended schedule. Take the following precautions to prevent rabies exposure: According to Florida Department of Health: Keep pets under direct supervision and on a leash, and keep livestock secured on your property. If an animal bites your pet or livestock, seek veterinary assistance for the animal immediately and contact Brevard County Sherrif’s Office Animal Services at 321-633-2024. Avoid contact with wild or stray animals. Do not handle, feed, or unintentionally attract them with outdoor pet food, open garbage cans, or other sources of food. If you have been bitten or scratched by a wild or domestic animal, seek medical attention, and report the injury to DOH-Brevard by calling 321-634-6337 option 3. Never adopt wild animals or bring them into your home; instead, contact a licensed wildlife rehabilitator. Prevent wildlife, including bats, from entering living quarters or occupied spaces in homes, schools, and other similar areas where they might come in contact with people and pets. For information on rabies, please visit FloridaHealth.gov/Rabies or contact DOH-Brevard at 321-634-6337 option 3. Contact Waymer at jwaymer@floridatoday.com. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabid Bat Found Dead Inside Snellville Home, Officials Can't Confirm Any Human Contact

News Brief
A rabid bat was found dead inside a home in Snellville, Georgia, on August 28, 2026. Gwinnett County officials confirmed the rabies test result but could not determine if any humans or pets had contact with the bat. The discovery has prompted a public advisory to avoid stray or unusual animals. While the bat's presence in the home raises concerns, there is no confirmed human exposure. Officials are investigating how the bat entered the home, and the situation remains under review. The county has reported a decline in confirmed animal rabies cases in recent years, but the incident underscores the ongoing risk of rabies from wildlife. Residents are advised to ensure their pets are up to date on vaccinations and to seek medical attention if exposed to a suspected rabid animal. The broader context shows that wildlife, particularly bats and raccoons, are significant sources of rabies in Georgia, with bats accounting for 2.7% of positive specimens in a state-wide study from 2010 to 2021.

Subscribe More forecasts: Atlanta 30 day forecast Atlanta/ Crime & Emergencies Rabid Bat Found Dead Inside Snellville Home, Officials Can't Confirm Any Human Contact By Ray Bianchi Published on September 05, 2026 Source: Google Street View A bat found dead inside a home near Stockton Walk Lane in Snellville has tested positive for rabies, prompting Gwinnett County officials to issue a public advisory urging residents to steer clear of stray or unusually behaving animals. The bat was discovered on August 28, and officials say they have not been able to confirm whether any humans or household pets came into direct contact with it before it died. The discovery, first reported by FOX 5 Atlanta, has left investigators without answers on a key question: how the bat got into the home in the first place. That detail remains undetermined, according to the outlet, which cited a press release from Gwinnett County government. Gwinnett County Animal Welfare and Enforcement issued the advisory alongside the confirmed test result, warning pet owners to keep their animals current on rabies vaccinations. Why Bats Are the Biggest Rabies Threat Bats and foxes are among the wild animals capable of carrying and transmitting the virus, and nationally, bats are responsible for roughly 70% of fatal human rabies cases acquired in the United States, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Rabies spreads to humans and pets through bites or scratches from infected wildlife, and once symptoms appear, the virus is almost always fatal in humans if preventive treatment isn't given immediately, per the CDC. The virus attacks the central nervous system, and early symptoms in people can include fever, headache, and general weakness or discomfort. That danger is part of why Gwinnett County Animal Welfare and Enforcement Director Katrina Hellman has previously urged residents to call animal control rather than pest control if a bat turns up inside a home, warning that a sleeping person could be bitten without ever feeling it or noticing a mark, according to WSB Radio. The county recorded 10 confirmed animal rabies cases in 2024, with a slight decrease in confirmed cases in 2025, per the same report. What to Do After a Possible Exposure Officials say anyone bitten or scratched by a stray or suspected rabid animal should seek medical care immediately and tell their healthcare provider about the exposure. The standard post-exposure regimen for unvaccinated people recommended by the CDC includes thorough wound washing, one dose of human rabies immune globulin, and four doses of rabies vaccine given on days 0, 3, 7, and 14. Pets face their own protocol. Unvaccinated dogs or cats exposed to a rabid animal must undergo a strict four-month quarantine and receive a rabies vaccination one month before release, according to NASPHV guidelines cited in the FOX 5 Atlanta report. Separately, Gwinnett County ordinances require any dog, cat, or ferret that bites or scratches a person to undergo a mandatory 10-day quarantine observation, per Gwinnett County. Georgia law also mandates that all dogs, cats, and ferrets 12 weeks or older be vaccinated against rabies by a licensed veterinarian, under Georgia Code § 31-19-5. Anyone who believes they were exposed to a suspected rabid animal can contact GNR Public Health at 770-339-4260. Residents who spot an animal behaving unusually, or who need one picked up, can call the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement bite office at 770-339-3200, ext. 5576. Part of a Wider Pattern Across Gwinnett The Snellville case is the latest in a string of wildlife rabies alerts across the county this year. In July, officials reported a raccoon near Biltmore Oaks Drive in Bethlehem had tested positive after attacking a dog in May, according to 11Alive. Hoodline has also tracked a rabid coyote in Buford and reported on a rabid raccoon that mauled a dog in Dacula. A retrospective study of Georgia rabies laboratory data from 2010 to 2021 found wildlife made up 93.7% of confirmed positive cases statewide, with raccoons accounting for 68.9% and bats making up 2.7% of positive specimens, according to a report published on PubMed Central. Domestic animals accounted for only 6.3% of positive submissions over that period, underscoring how mandatory pet vaccination laws have shifted the state's rabies risk almost entirely onto wildlife. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Kitten in Jacksonville, raccoon in Taneytown test positive for rabies, health officials warn - WBAL-TV

News Brief
Health officials in Maryland and Florida have reported two rabies cases: a raccoon in Taneytown and a kitten in Jacksonville. The raccoon was found aggressive on August 27, 2026, and was killed by a dog. Officials are concerned about potential exposure to other pets or people in the area. In Baltimore County, a kitten tested positive for rabies on August 27, 2026, and there was one human exposure. The health department is notifying caretakers of community cat colonies and advising immediate medical evaluation for anyone who may have had contact with the infected animals. Rabies is a preventable disease when exposures are reported promptly and vaccination practices are followed. Officials are also organizing a low-cost rabies vaccination clinic to help prevent further spread. The public is urged to avoid contact with wild or stray animals and ensure their pets are up to date on vaccinations.
Original source text (English)
Kitten in Jacksonville, raccoon in Taneytown test positive for rabies, health officials warn Rabies virus, illustration. Rabies virus is a member of the Rhabdoviridae family. This bullet-shaped enveloped virus has a protein coat (capsid), which is made up of helically arranged subunits called capsomeres and covered with surface glycoprotein spikes. Within the coat is the genetic material in the form of single-stranded ribonucleic acid (RNA). The rabies virus is transmitted to humans from an infected animal bite. Symptoms appear after an incubation period of 10 days to 1 year and include fever, breathing difficulties, muscle spasms, and hydrophobia SOURCE: Kateryna Kon/Science Photo Library via Getty Images Advertisement Updated: 2:26 PM EDT Sep 2, 2026 Editorial Standards i Greg Ng Digital Content Manager Kitten in Jacksonville, raccoon in Taneytown test positive for rabies, health officials warn Updated: 2:26 PM EDT Sep 2, 2026 Editorial Standards i Greg Ng Digital Content Manager TANEYTOWN, Md. — A raccoon in the Taneytown area and a kitten in Jacksonville tested positive for rabies , health officials announced Wednesday. Carroll County health officials said Animal Control was called to York Street near Maryland Route 140 on Aug. 27 for an aggressive raccoon that was killed by a dog. Advertisement Health officials said they're concerned that other pets or people in the area may have been exposed to the infected raccoon before its death. "Rabies exposure occurs through bites and scratches or saliva from the infected animal getting into the eyes, nose, mouth, or a wound of a person or another animal," Joe Mancuso, the Carroll County Health Department's rabies program manager, said in a statement. Anyone who may have been exposed or whose pet may have been exposed to bites, scratches, or saliva from this raccoon can call the health department at 410-876-1884 for a risk assessment. In Baltimore County, health officials announced that a kitten found in the 3300 block of Paper Mill Road tested positive Tuesday for rabies. The animal was an approximately 16‐week‐old orange domestic medium-haired male. Baltimore County Animal Services said there was one human exposure and officials are notifying caretakers of registered community cat colonies in the area. The Baltimore County Department of Health should be contacted if direct exposure occurred involving bites, scratches, or licks. Call 410‐887‐5963 during business hours, or 410‐832‐7182 after hours. Immediate medical evaluation is recommended for anyone who may have had contact with the affected animals. "Rabies is a preventable disease, but only when exposures are reported promptly and vaccination practices are followed," BCAS Bureau Director Dr. René Varela said in a statement. "Incidents like this highlight the importance of avoiding contact with unfamiliar animals and ensuring that pets remain current on their rabies vaccinations. Maintaining vigilance in the community is essential to protecting both animal and human health." Health officials offered the following information to help keep yourself, your family and your pets safe from rabies. Do not approach, handle, pet or feed wild or stray animals. Have your dogs, cats and ferrets vaccinated against rabies and keep their vaccinations up to date. Do not leave pets outside unattended or allow them to roam free. Cover garbage cans tightly and do not leave pet food outside. Teach children to stay away from wild animals and any animals that they do not know. Prevent bats from entering your home by using window screens and chimney caps. Bats found in the home should be safely collected, if possible, and tested for rabies. The Carroll County Health Department's next low-cost rabies vaccination clinic is scheduled for 2-4 p.m. on Sept. 20 at the Ag Center in Westminster. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

喀斯喀特县发现患有狂犬病的蝙蝠 - The Electric

Rabid bat found in Cascade County - The Electric
新闻简报2026年9月1日,蒙大拿州卡斯卡德县发现一只狂犬病感染的蝙蝠,并由卡斯卡德市-县卫生部门(CCHD)确认。该蝙蝠被发现靠近一只当地的宠物猫,引发了对猫可能被抓伤或咬伤的担忧。猫的主人带了已接种疫苗的猫去兽医处进行狂犬病加强针接种,并同时将蝙蝠送去检测。卫生官员强调,宠物只能在死后进行狂犬病检测,且狂犬病状态对于下一步行动至关重要。尽管美国狂犬病致人死亡的情况较为罕见,自1990年代以来平均每年发生1至2起,但预防性狂犬病疫苗接种成功率接近100%。CCHD建议避免与野生动物接触,尤其是蝙蝠,不要喂养或处理它们。如果一个人被无法追踪的动物咬伤,可能需要接受一系列狂犬病疫苗接种。该事件突显了在该地区进行狂犬病预防和意识提升的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年9月1日,卡斯卡德县发现一只蝙蝠检测出狂犬病阳性。2025年9月28日,猫的主人带了已接种疫苗的猫去兽医处进行狂犬病加强针接种,并同时将蝙蝠送去检测。该蝙蝠由兽医送检。自1990年代以来,美国狂犬病致人死亡的情况较为罕见,平均每年发生1至2起。蒙大拿州卡斯卡德县最后一次记录的狂犬病致人死亡发生在1997年。
地点与对象
这起事件发生在蒙大拿州喀斯喀特县,涉及当地一只宠物猫和一只蝙蝠。喀斯喀特市县卫生部门 (CCHD) 的卫生官员参与了应对工作。
事件
喀斯喀特县发现了一只患有狂犬病的蝙蝠,卫生官员证实了 狂犬病 检测呈阳性。这只蝙蝠是在当地一只宠物猫附近发现的,人们担心这只猫被蝙蝠抓伤或咬伤。猫主人将接种疫苗的猫带到兽医处注射 狂犬病 加强针,并带来了球棒进行测试。
规模、影响与风险
The incident involves a single rabid bat and a potential exposure to a pet cat. Rabies is a fatal disease, and preventive treatment is nearly 100 percent successful. If pets have not been vaccinated and are potentially exposed to rabies, they could be put down. The Centers for Disease Control reports that human rabies deaths in the U.S.
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员提醒当地人注意接触 狂犬病 的风险。他们建议不要喂养或处理野生动物,尤其是蝙蝠,并避免被家畜咬伤。如果某人被无法定位、观察或测试的动物咬伤,则可能需要接受一系列注射以预防 狂犬病。宠物只能在死后(即动物死亡后)进行 狂犬病 检测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabid bat found in Cascade County - The Electric

News Brief
A rabid bat was found in Cascade County, Montana, on September 1, 2026, and confirmed by the Cascade City-County Health Department (CCHD). The bat was discovered near a local pet cat, raising concerns that the cat may have been scratched or bitten. The cat owner took the vaccinated cat to the vet for a rabies booster and also brought the bat for testing. Health officials emphasized that pets can only be tested for rabies post-mortem and that vaccination status is crucial in determining next steps. While human rabies deaths in the U.S. are rare, averaging one to two per year since the 1990s, preventive treatment is nearly 100% successful. The CCHD recommends avoiding contact with wild animals, especially bats, and not feeding or handling them. If a person is bitten by an untraceable animal, they may need a series of rabies shots. The incident highlights the importance of rabies prevention and awareness in the region.
Original source text (English)
Rabid bat found in Cascade County Posted on September 1, 2026 by Jenn Rowell A bat in Cascade County tested positive for rabies, and health officials are reminding locals to be aware of the risk for rabies exposure. On Aug. 28, the Cascade City-County Health Department confirmed the positive rabies test. Trisha Gardner, county health officer, told The Electric that the bat was found near a local pet cat and there was concern that the cat had killed the bat, or otherwise been scratched or bitten by the bat. Pets can become infected by biting bats, she said. Bird flu detected in Gibson Park goose; waterfowl roundup postponed [2025] The cat owner took the vaccinated cat to the vet for a rabies booster shot and also brought the bat, which the vet sent off for testing, Gardner said. Pets can only be tested for rabies post-mortem, which means after the animal has died. Gardner said that in incidents involving pets and bats, they check the pet’s vaccination status to determine next steps. If the animal is vaccinated, no further action is typically needed. CCHD confirms rabid bat in Cascade County [2024] If an animal is not vaccinated, health officials may quarantine or euthanize it, depending on the circumstances. Gardner said it’s difficult to see bat bites on humans, and even more difficult to see bat bites or scratches on animals. Rabies is a fatal disease. The rabies virus is carried in the saliva of infected warm-blooded mammals and is usually transmitted to people and other animals through a bite. FWP detects fungus in bats in Cascade County cave [2023] Human rabies deaths in the U.S. are rare, according to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control, and average one to two deaths per year since the 1990s. Preventive treatment for rabies is nearly 100 percent successful; the last identified human death in Montana occurred in 1997. Rabid bat confirmed in Cascade County [2021] Rabies is preventable and CCHD recommends following these tips: do not feed or handle wild animals, especially bats. Bats and skunks are the most likely carriers of the rabies virus in Montana and should be completely avoided. Bats are especially concerning because their teeth are so small that a bite may not be noticeable. avoid animal bites from domestic animals. Never approach an animal at large and always ask the owner’s permission before petting an animal. Another common source of bite exposures is attempting to help a sick or injured wild animal. vaccinate dogs and cats against rabies. Cats are especially susceptible to rabies, and all dogs and cats should have a current rabies certificate. If pets have not been vaccinated and are potentially exposed to rabies, they could be put down. bat-proof your house. Bats must not be allowed in living areas of your home. Put screens on all windows, doors, and chimneys to prevent bat entry. know what to do if you catch a suspected animal: if you do catch a bat, or other animal suspected of having rabies, do your best not to damage the head. The brain is needed for the rabies test. know what to do if there’s a bite. If someone is bitten by a domestic dog, cat, or ferret, the animal can be observed for signs of rabies, almost always avoiding the need for treatment. If you are bitten by an animal that cannot be located, observed, or tested, a person may need to undergo a series of shots to prevent rabies. If you are bitten, call a health provider or CCHD immediately. Cascade County Great Falls Health Public Safety

Rabies Alert: Did you visit this NC mobile petting zoo? - WECT

时间
该警报于 2026 年 8 月 31 日得到确认,潜在暴露时间可追溯到 2026 年 7 月 28 日。

北卡罗来纳州的几个县,包括哈莫尼县、斯泰茨维尔县、列克星敦县、莫克斯维尔县,以及梅克伦堡县、沃托加县、伯克县、达勒姆县和艾雷德尔县等其他县。

一家流动宠物动物园的三只小山羊检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性,第四只推测被感染。卫生官员正在向与会者通报山羊可能受到感染的活动。
规模、影响与风险
山羊确诊三例,疑似病例一例。如果不治疗,狂犬病 是致命的,但可以在症状出现前通过药物预防。官员们正在努力识别并通知可能接触过病毒的个人。

调查正在进行中,官员们正在与活动组织者合作通知与会者。正在向可能接触过受感染山羊的人提供预防性 治疗。

Rabies Alert: Did you visit this NC mobile petting zoo? - WECT

News Brief
A rabies alert has been issued in several North Carolina counties after three baby goats at a mobile petting zoo tested positive for the virus, with a fourth presumed infected. The North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services and the Department of Agriculture & Consumer Services confirmed the cases, stating the goats were likely exposed by a skunk that entered their pen in late July. Health officials are contacting individuals who may have had contact with the goats at events between July 28 and August 27, 2026, to determine if preventative treatment is necessary. Rabies is fatal if left untreated but can be prevented with medication before symptoms begin. The investigation is ongoing, and additional information about potential exposure locations will be released as it becomes available. No other animals on the farm are believed to pose a risk.

An urgent rabies alert is active across several North Carolina counties after three baby goats at a mobile petting zoo tested positive for the virus. By Jada Harris Published: Aug. 31, 2026 at 2:23 PM UTC Share Add Us On GoogleAdd as a preferred source on Google RALEIGH, N.C. (WECT) - An urgent rabies alert is active across several North Carolina counties after three baby goats at a mobile petting zoo tested positive for the virus. The North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services and the Department of Agriculture & Consumer Services announced three baby goats from Farm Adventures in Harmony tested positive for the virus, with a fourth also presumed to be infected. Health officials said the goats were likely exposed to the virus by a skunk that entered their pen in late July. Officials added no other animals on the farm are believed to pose a risk. NCDHHS officials explained they learned of the potential exposure on Aug. 27, and the NC State Laboratory of Public Health confirmed the presence of the rabies virus in three of the baby goats on Aug. 28. NCDHHS officials ask anyone who had contact with the goats at the Harmony location or at off-site events between July 28 and Aug. 27 to contact their local health department to determine if preventative treatment is necessary. Rabies is fatal if left untreated, but preventable if medication is administered before symptoms begin. Public exposure sites span several counties and include Jurney’s Assisted Living in Statesville (July 28), the Back-to-School Bash in Lexington (Aug. 1), Brookdale Peachtree in Statesville (Aug. 6), and the Masonic Picnic Grounds in Mocksville (Aug. 15), alongside several private events in Mecklenburg, Watauga, Burke, Durham, and Iredell counties. Public health officials confirm they are working with event organizers to notify attendees who may have come into contact with the infected goats at these events. The investigation is ongoing and additional information about potential exposure locations at public events will be released when available. Human rabies infections are extremely rare in the U.S., with North Carolina’s last reported human case occurring in 2011. For more information, click here. Copyright 2026 WECT. All rights reserved. 公园证实,约塞米蒂国家公园咖喱村的死蝙蝠 狂犬病 检测呈阳性 - 哥伦比亚广播公司新闻

Dead bat tests positive for rabies at Yosemite National Park's Curry Village, park confirms - CBS News
新闻简报优胜美地国家公园证实,2026 年 5 月 18 日在咖喱村发现的一只死蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测结果呈阳性。这是继 2025 年 7 月发生一起病例之后,2026 年该公园报告的第二例 狂犬病 阳性蝙蝠。官员强调,虽然 狂犬病 是一种罕见但严重的疾病,但对人类的风险非常低,因为公园里只有一小部分蝙蝠被感染。建议可能接触过蝙蝠的游客咨询加州公共卫生部 (CDPH) 或其医疗保健提供者。公园重申警告不要触摸或处理蝙蝠,并指出即使是像用刷子刷翅膀或皮肤这样的轻微接触也可能造成风险。目前尚未报告人间病例,情况仍在调查中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 5 月 18 日,在优胜美地国家公园的咖喱村发现了一只死蝙蝠。公园在 2026 年 9 月 3 日星期三的一份声明中确认了 狂犬病 测试结果。
地点与对象
优胜美地国家公园,特别是咖喱村,是深受游客欢迎的地区。受影响人群包括2026年5月18日左右可能在该地区与蝙蝠有过直接接触的游客。
事件
约塞米蒂国家公园官员证实,一只死去的大棕色蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性。根据公园的声明和测试结果,这是已确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
这是一例蝙蝠感染 狂犬病 的确诊病例。目前还没有人类病例的报道,官员们表示,这种病毒对人类的风险非常低,因为每年公园里只有极少数蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dead bat tests positive for rabies at Yosemite National Park's Curry Village, park confirms - CBS News

News Brief
Yosemite National Park confirmed that a dead bat found in Curry Village on May 18, 2026, tested positive for rabies. This is the second rabies-positive bat reported in the park in 2026, following a case in July 2025. Officials emphasized that while rabies is a rare but serious disease, the risk to humans is very low, as only a small percentage of bats in the park are infected. Visitors who may have had contact with the bat are advised to consult the California Department of Public Health (CDPH) or their healthcare provider. The park reiterated its warning against touching or handling bats, noting that even minor contact like a brush with a wing or skin can pose a risk. No human cases have been reported, and the situation remains under investigation.

Read Full Bio Tim Fang September 3, 2026 / 8:29 AM PDT / CBS Sacramento Add CBS News on Google Officials at Yosemite National Park said that a bat found dead in Yosemite Valley earlier this year had rabies. In a statement Wednesday, park officials confirmed that a "dead big brown bat" tested positive. The bat was found outside the Curry Village Showerhouse on May 18. Officials said no human contact with the infected bat has been reported. Visitors who may have had direct contact with a bat in Curry Village around that time are urged to contact the California Department of Public Health (CDPH) and their healthcare provider. "Bat bites and scratches can be very small and may not always be visible or painful. Direct contact with a bat also includes on top of clothing or if a wing brushes the skin or hair," park officials said. The bat was the second one in a year to have tested positive for rabies in Yosemite, with the last rabies-positive bat being discovered in July 2025. Officials said a "very small percentage" of bats tested from the park each year have rabies. Yosemite is home to 17 species of bats, according to the park. Park officials urged visitors not to touch or handle bats or other wild animals. Anyone who encounters a bat, dead or alive, should keep their distance and notify park staff. A rare but serious disease, rabies is caused by the rabies virus, which infects the brain. People and animals almost always die once symptoms begin, but the disease is preventable if one seeks medical care right away. According to CDPH, rabies has been reported in 17 people in California since 1980, with the most recent case in 2024. 在雇用了受感染山羊的公司后,妈妈公开谈论可能的 狂犬病 - ABC News - 突发新闻、最新新闻和视频

After hiring company with infected goats, mom speaks out on possible rabies exposure - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报北卡罗来纳州的一位母亲艾琳·克鲁兹 (Aylin Cruz) 在雇用了一家移动宠物动物园公司 Farm Adventures 后发表了自己的看法,该公司的山羊 狂犬病 检测呈阳性。这些山羊很可能是因为进入围栏的臭鼬而接触到病毒的。2026 年 7 月 28 日至 8 月 27 日期间举行的聚会几天后,克鲁兹就收到了有关 狂犬病 潜在暴露的警报。州官员确认这些山羊的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,并警告可能存在人类暴露,尽管自 2011 年以来没有报告病例。他们建议如果怀疑暴露,请联系当地卫生部门。该公司老板表示,所有动物和工人都已接受了 狂犬病 加强剂,但情况仍在调查中。这一事件凸显了通过动物接触传播 狂犬病 传播 的风险以及及时采取医疗行动的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件发生在2026年7月28日至8月27日期间,新闻报道于2026年9月2日发布。
地点与对象
该事件发生在美国北卡罗来纳州,涉及一家移动宠物动物园公司及其山羊,这些山羊可能暴露于狂犬病,并且可能在活动期间与人接触过。
事件
北卡罗来纳州的一位母亲艾琳·克鲁兹 (Aylin Cruz) 雇佣了一家移动宠物动物园公司 Farm Adventures,该公司的山羊 狂犬病 检测呈阳性。事件发生后,她被警告可能暴露于 狂犬病。
规模、影响与风险
Farm Adventures 移动宠物动物园的四只山羊可能被臭鼬接触到 狂犬病。这些山羊可能在2026年7月28日至8月27日期间与人类有过直接接触,引发了人们对潜在暴露的担忧。州官员指出,自 2011 年以来,北卡罗来纳州没有出现人类 狂犬病 病例,但建议如果怀疑有接触史,请联系当地卫生部门。
应对、状态与不确定性
州官员已确认这些山羊的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,并正在调查此次暴露情况。该公司老板表示,所有动物和工人都已收到 狂犬病 加强剂。目前尚未报告确诊的人类病例,但卫生部门敦促人们如果接触过病毒,请寻求 疫苗接种。

After hiring company with infected goats, mom speaks out on possible rabies exposure - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
A North Carolina mother, Aylin Cruz, has spoken out after hiring a mobile petting zoo company, Farm Adventures, which had goats that tested positive for rabies. The goats were likely exposed to the virus by a skunk that entered their pen. Cruz was alerted to the potential rabies exposure a few days after the party, which took place between July 28 and August 27, 2026. State officials confirmed the goats tested positive for rabies and warned of possible human exposure, though no cases have been reported since 2011. They advised contacting local health departments if exposure is suspected. The company owner stated all animals and workers have received rabies boosters, but the situation remains under investigation. This incident highlights the risk of rabies transmission through animal contact and the importance of prompt medical action.

北卡罗来纳州宠物动物园游客被警告 3只小山羊检测出狂犬病

North Carolina Petting Zoo Visitors Warned After 3 Baby Goats Test Positive for Rabies
新闻简报在北卡罗来纳州,一家流动宠物动物园的三只小山羊检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性,其中第四只被认为受到感染。七月下旬,当一只臭鼬进入山羊圈时,这些山羊就接触到了病毒。北卡罗来纳州卫生与公众服务部 (NCDHHS) 确定了 7 个县的 10 起潜在暴露事件,包括公共和私人集会。虽然家畜感染 狂犬病 的风险低于野生动物,但接触受感染动物的唾液仍可能构成威胁。NCDHHS 敦促任何可能接触过病毒的人联系当地卫生部门进行评估并采取可能的预防措施 治疗。公共卫生工作者正在与活动组织者和参加者联系,以评估潜在风险并确保采取适当的后续行动。该机构强调,症状可能需要数周或数月的时间才会出现,因此迅速采取行动对于防止进一步传播至关重要。
查看新闻五要素
时间
北卡罗来纳州卫生与公众服务部根据 8 月 28 日收集的信息确定了风险,这些山羊是在 7 月 28 日至 8 月 28 日之间暴露的。
地点与对象
该活动涉及北卡罗来纳州的一个移动宠物动物园,特别是和谐农场冒险,并影响了梅克伦堡、沃托加、伯克、达勒姆和艾雷德尔等七个县的人们。
事件
Farm Adventures 的三只小山羊的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,第四只被认为已被感染。七月下旬,当一只臭鼬进入山羊圈时,这些山羊就接触到了病毒。
规模、影响与风险
NCDHHS 确定了 10 起潜在暴露事件,涉及可能在公共和私人活动中与山羊互动的人。来自家畜的 狂犬病 传播 的风险低于来自野生动物的风险,但接触受感染动物的唾液仍可能构成风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生工作人员正在联系可能与这些动物有过互动的组织者和参加者。卫生官员建议,如果发生接触,请联系当地卫生部门,并且在接触其他人之前可能需要预防性药物治疗。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

North Carolina Petting Zoo Visitors Warned After 3 Baby Goats Test Positive for Rabies

News Brief
In North Carolina, three baby goats from a mobile petting zoo tested positive for rabies, with a fourth assumed to be infected. The goats were exposed to the virus when a skunk entered their pen in late July. The North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services (NCDHHS) identified 10 potential exposure events across seven counties, including public and private gatherings. While the risk of rabies from livestock is lower than from wild animals, contact with saliva from infected animals can still pose a threat. NCDHHS is urging anyone who may have been exposed to contact their local health department for evaluation and possible preventive treatment. Public health workers are reaching out to event organizers and attendees to assess potential risks and ensure proper follow-up. The agency emphasized that symptoms may take weeks or months to appear, so prompt action is critical to prevent further spread.
Original source text (English)
Anyone who recently held, fed or cuddled baby goats from a mobile petting zoo in North Carolina may have been exposed to rabies. Three of the goats tested positive, while a fourth was not tested but is assumed to have been infected. The goats came from Farm Adventures in Harmony, North Carolina. They were also taken to several public and private events across the state between July 28 and Aug. 27. The North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services believes a skunk entered the goats' pen in late July and exposed them to the virus. Farm Adventures said the goats were too young to receive rabies vaccinations and showed no symptoms. The four goats, named Molly, Maggie, Cow and Uno, were euthanized (as reported on a Facebook post that is on longer available). Based on the information gathered on Aug. 28, health officials determined that no other animals at the farm pose a rabies exposure risk to people. NCDHHS has identified 10 potential exposure events in seven counties. The list includes assisted living facilities in Statesville, a back-to-school event in Lexington and a community event at the Masonic Picnic Grounds in Mocksville. The goats also attended private events in Mecklenburg, Watauga, Burke, Durham and Iredell counties. Public health workers are contacting organizers and attendees who may have interacted with the animals. Anyone who visited Farm Adventures or came into contact with its baby goats at an event between July 28 and Aug. 27 should contact their local health department. Rabies can spread through a bite or scratch. It can also spread if saliva from an infected animal gets into someone's eyes, nose or mouth or touches broken skin. The risk of catching rabies from livestock is lower than the risk from a wild animal bite. However, baby goats are often bottle-fed, carried and cuddled, which could bring people into contact with their saliva. Human rabies is extremely rare in the United States, and North Carolina has not reported a human case since 2011. NCDHHS is asking anyone who may have been exposed to call their local health department before visiting an emergency room or urgent care clinic. This way, staff can evaluate the person's risk and explain whether preventive medication is needed before exposing others. Rabies is almost always fatal once symptoms begin, but it can be prevented when treatment starts before that happens. Symptoms can take weeks or months to appear, so people should not wait until they feel sick before calling. Want more articles like this one? 受感染的蝙蝠在著名营地被发现死亡数月后,狂犬病 案件曝光,优胜美地游客愤怒不已 - nypost.com

Yosemite visitors rage after rabies case revealed months after infected bat found dead at famed camp - nypost.com
新闻简报优胜美地国家公园官员透露,2026 年 5 月发现的一只死亡蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,但他们直到 2026 年 9 月 2 日才通知公众。这只蝙蝠是在公园内的热门区域库里村发现的。国家公园管理局强调,目前尚无人类与蝙蝠接触的情况,也没有游客中报告 狂犬病 感染病例。不过,他们敦促任何可能接触过蝙蝠的人立即就医。如果没有立即 狂犬病,狂犬病 几乎总是致命的,公园正在通过对死亡蝙蝠的常规检测来监测野生动物疾病。信息披露的延迟引起了游客的担忧,他们质疑公园反应的透明度。
查看新闻五要素
时间
国家公园管理局于 2026 年 9 月 2 日星期四向公众通报了 狂犬病 案件。这只蝙蝠于 2026 年 5 月被发现死亡。
地点与对象
优胜美地国家公园,特别是加利福尼亚州的咖喱村。受影响的人群包括可能接触过蝙蝠的游客。
事件
优胜美地国家公园官员透露,2026 年 5 月发现的一只死亡蝙蝠的 狂犬病 检测呈阳性,但他们直到 2026 年 9 月 2 日才通知公众。这是由国家公园管理局报道的。
规模、影响与风险
目前尚无人类与蝙蝠接触的情况,也未报告游客中出现 狂犬病 感染病例。国家公园管理局敦促任何可能接触过蝙蝠的人寻求医疗救助。
应对、状态与不确定性
国家公园管理局敦促可能接触过蝙蝠的个人联系加州卫生部和医疗保健提供者。回应正在进行中,情况正在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Yosemite visitors rage after rabies case revealed months after infected bat found dead at famed camp - nypost.com

News Brief
Yosemite National Park officials faced public outrage after revealing that a bat found dead in May 2026 had tested positive for rabies, but they only notified the public on September 2, 2026. The bat was discovered at Curry Village, a popular area in the park. The National Park Service emphasized that no human contact with the bat is known, and no cases of rabies infection have been reported among visitors. However, they are urging anyone who may have had contact with the bat to seek medical attention promptly. Rabies is almost always fatal without immediate treatment, and the park is monitoring wildlife diseases through routine testing of deceased bats. The delay in disclosure has raised concerns among visitors, who questioned the transparency of the park's response.
Original source text (English)

狂犬病 确认来自旅行宠物动物园的小山羊,官员敦促游客进行检查

Rabies Confirmed in Baby Goats From Traveling Petting Zoo, Officials Urge Visitors to Get Checked
新闻简报北卡罗来纳州的一家移动宠物动物园 Farm Adventures 在四只小山羊检测出 狂犬病 呈阳性后正在接受审查。这些动物很可能是因为七月下旬进入它们围栏的臭鼬而接触到了病毒。官员们已经确认了三例病例,并假设第四例被感染,导致所有四只山羊被安乐死,以防止进一步传播。暴露风险涉及多个地点,包括达勒姆县、斯泰茨维尔、列克星敦、沃托加县、伯克县和莫克斯维尔,其中 10 个地点可能受到影响。公共卫生当局敦促任何与山羊接触过的人寻求医疗评估,因为即使是唾液接触也会带来风险。农场冒险建立了一个呼叫中心来帮助关心的游客。虽然自 2011 年以来北卡罗来纳州没有报告人类 狂犬病 病例,但对于那些暴露的人来说,预防性 狂犬病 是可用的。这一事件凸显了对动物保持警惕的重要性,即使是那些看起来无害的动物。

时间
该事件发生在 7 月下旬至 8 月期间,发布日期为 2026 年 9 月 1 日。

应对、状态与不确定性

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabies Confirmed in Baby Goats From Traveling Petting Zoo, Officials Urge Visitors to Get Checked

News Brief
A mobile petting zoo in North Carolina, Farm Adventures, is under scrutiny after four baby goats tested positive for rabies. The animals were likely exposed to the virus by a skunk that entered their pen in late July. Officials have confirmed three cases and assumed the fourth to be infected, leading to the euthanasia of all four goats to prevent further spread. The exposure risk spans multiple locations, including Durham County, Statesville, Lexington, Watauga County, Burke County, and Mocksville, with 10 sites potentially affected. Public health authorities are urging anyone who had contact with the goats to seek medical evaluation, as even saliva contact poses a risk. Farm Adventures has established a call center to assist concerned visitors. While no human rabies cases have been reported in North Carolina since 2011, preventive treatment is available for those exposed. The incident highlights the importance of vigilance with animals, even those that appear harmless.
Original source text (English)
A mobile petting zoo in North Carolina is at the center of a growing health scare after four of its baby goats tested positive for rabies, and officials are urging anyone who touched them to seek medical guidance immediately. The goats belonged to Farm Adventures, a traveling petting zoo based in Harmony, North Carolina. According to the North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services, the goats were most likely exposed to rabies by a skunk that entered their pen in late July. Lab testing found the virus in three of the four baby goats, and the fourth was also assumed to have been infected. All four were sadly euthanized to prevent the virus from spreading further. 🐶 SIGN UP to get "pawsitivity" delivered right to your inbox with inspiring & entertaining stories about our furry & feathered friends 🐾🐾 The goats reportedly had sweet, ordinary names—Molly, Maggie, Cow, and Uno—which make the situation feel even more heartbreaking for anyone who may have bottle-fed or cuddled them during a visit. The concern extends well beyond the zoo's home base, as the goats traveled to multiple North Carolina communities, including two stops in Durham County, as well as Statesville, Lexington, Watauga County, Burke County and Mocksville, all between July 28 and Aug. 27. Exposure may have happened at 10 different locations, including an assisted living facility and a back-to-school event. Related: Baby Pygmy Hippo Born at Edinburgh Zoo Is the Roundest Little Meatball Rabies is nearly always fatal once symptoms appear, which is why health officials are treating this seriously. Because baby goats are frequently bottle-fed, handled closely or cuddled, public health officials are working proactively to ensure anyone who has had direct contact with the affected animals is evaluated, since even saliva contact carries some risk of exposure. The good news is that North Carolina has not reported a human rabies case since 2011, and treatment given before symptoms develop is highly effective at preventing infection. Anyone who visited Farm Adventures or one of its off-site events during the exposure window is encouraged to contact their local health department to determine whether preventive treatment is needed. Farm Adventures has also set up a call center at 336-242-2371 to help answer questions from concerned visitors. For pet and animal lovers, this story is a good reminder that even the cuddliest, most harmless-looking animals can carry serious risks, and that a quick call to a health official is always worth it if there's any doubt.
